Global small solutions of MHD boundary layer equations in Gevrey function space
Zhong Tan, Zhonger Wu

Abstract
. In this paper, we obtain global small solutions and decay estimates for the MHD boundary layer in Gevrey space without any structural assumptions, generalizing the results of [ 15 ] in analytic space. The proof method is mainly inspired by [ 19 ] and [ 26 ] , using new auxiliary functions and finer structural analysis to overcome the difficulty of the loss of derivatives and then we obtain the global well-posedness of the MHD boundary layer in the Gevrey 3 2 \frac{3}{2} space.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
35Q35; 76W05; 76D10.

1.
Introduction

In this paper, we study the well-posedness of the two dimensional MHD boundary layer in Gevrey space in the upper half-space
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}=\{(x,y)|x\in\mathbb{R},y\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\}
.The equations take the following form (see
[
9
,
11
,
14
]
):

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\left(\partial_{t}+u\partial_{x}+v\partial_{y}-\partial_{y}^{2}\right)u-\left(b\partial_{x}+h\partial_{y}\right)b+\partial_{x}p=0\\
\partial_{t}b+\partial_{y}(vb-uh)-\kappa\partial_{y}^{2}b=0\\
\partial_{t}h-\partial_{x}(vb-uh)-\kappa\partial_{y}^{2}h=0\end{array}\right.

with the divergence-free and homogeneous Dirichlet boundary conditions,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{x}u+\partial_{y}v=\partial_{x}b+\partial_{y}h=0,\\
(u,v,b,h)|_{y=0}=(0,0,0,0),~(u,b)|_{y\rightarrow+\infty}=(\overline{u},\overline{b}),\\
(u,b)|_{t=0}=(u_{0},b_{0}).\end{array}\right.

where
(u,v)
represent the velocity of fluid and
(b,h)
represent the magnetic field. And
\kappa>0
is a constant that represents the ratio between Reynolds number and magnetic Reynolds number. Furthermore
(\overline{u},\overline{b},p)
represent the traces of the tangential field and the pressure of the outflow on the boundary, satisfying Bernoulli’s law:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\overline{u}+\overline{u}\partial_{x}\overline{u}+\partial_{x}p=\overline{b}\partial_{x}\overline{b},\\
\partial_{t}\overline{b}+\overline{u}\partial_{x}\overline{b}=\overline{b}\partial_{x}\overline{u}.\end{array}\right.

When the magnetic field
(b,h)=(0,0)
, the equation (
1
) degenerates to the classical Prandtl equation. The Prandtl equation was originally proposed by Prandtl in 1904 to explain the difference between an ideal fluid and a low viscosity fluid near the boundary. The Prandtl equation has been studied for a long time, and a lot of results have been obtained. Under the assumption of monotonicity, Oleinik
[
23
]
proved the uniqueness of the local existence of classical solution using the Crocco transform. And in 2015,
[
1
]
and
[
22
]
independently proved similar results in weighted Sobolev space by energy methods. Under the favourable pressure condition, Zhouping Xin and Liqun Zhang
[
28
]
obtained the global existence of the weak solution, which is due to the favourable pressure condition to avoid the back-flow.

When the velocity has no monotonicity assumption, the Prandtl equation cannot overcome the derivative loss in the space of finite regularity, so it needs to be studied in the space of smooth functions. When the data is analytic in both the
x
variable and
y
variable, Sammartino and Caflisch
[
25
]
obtained for the first time the local well-posedness. Later Lombardo, Cannone and Sammartino
[
21
]

removed the analyticity requirement from the
y
variable.
Further relaxation of the analyticity conditions, we can refer to
[
4
,
6
,
8
,
17
,
20
]
for the well-posedness results in Gevrey space. In particular, without any structural assumptions,
[
6
]
obtained the local existence and uniqueness of the solution of the two dimensional Prandtl equation in the Gevrey 2 space, and
[
17
]
extended this result to the three dimensional case.The Gevrey index 2 is optimal in the sense of the ill-posedness result of
[
7
]
.
These results are all local. Regarding the large time results, Ping Zhang and Zhifei Zhang
[
29
]
first obtained the long-time existence of the solution of the Pandtl equation with small analytic data, and then
[
10
]
obtained the almost global existence of the solution. Finally, the global existence and decay estimates of the solution are obtained in
[
24
]
. Recently, Ping Zhang et al.
[
26
]
extended the results in
[
24
]
to the Gevrey 2 space.

Back to the MHD boundary layer. Under the structural assumption of the dominance of the tangential magnetic field, see the well-posedness results of
[
12
,
13
,
18
]
in Sobolev space. The comparison of these results with those of the classical Prandtl equation under the assumption of monotonicity shows that the magnetic field has a stabilizing effect on the MHD boundary layer regardless of the presence of resistance.
On the other hand without imposing structural assumptions, Feng Xie
and Tong Yang
[
27
]
obtained a lower bound on the existence time of the analytic solution of the equation (
1
). This result was subsequently improved by Ping Zhang et al.
[
15
]
to obtain the global existence of the analytic solution. In addition, Feng Xie et al.
[
16
]
further improved the result of
[
15
]
by removing the smallness requirement of the outflow at the initial moment.
In the Gevrey space, Weixi Li and Tong Yang
[
19
]
obtained the local well-posedness of (
1
) when the Gevrey indicator is
\frac{3}{2}
without imposing any structural assumptions. It is worth noting that, combined with the ill-posedness results in
[
14
]
, we can guess that the optimal Gevrey indicator for the MHD boundary layer (
1
) should be one of the numbers in the interval
[\frac{3}{2},2]
.

Inspired by
[
19
]
and
[
26
]
, we investigate the global well-posedness of (
1
)-(
1
) with small initial data, which is Gevrey
\frac{3}{2}
regular in the
x
variable and Sobolev regular in the
y
variable. For the sake of brevity of the proof process, we shall assume without loss of generality

(1.9)

\displaystyle(\overline{u},\overline{b},p)=(0,0,C),~\kappa\in(0,2).

In fact, for general
(\overline{u},\overline{b})
with smallness and
\kappa\in(\frac{1}{2},\infty)
, see the treatment in
[
15
]
.

The remainder of this paper is organized as follows: in Section
2
, we will give our main theorem, and some frequently used lemmas. In Section
3
, we list some main a priori estimates and give the proof of theorem
2.1
. In section
4
-
11
, we will prove all the a priori estimates listed in section
3
.

2.
Main Result and Preliminary

In this section we will first give an introduction on the basic notation in the paper, then give our main theorem, and finally list some useful lemmas.

2.1.
Notation

First we give an introduction to the basic notation in the paper.

First
a\lesssim b
means there exists a unform positive constant
C
(the constant may be different between each line) such that
a\leq Cb
holds. Let
\langle t\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}1+t
,
[\xi]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}(1+\xi^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
. And
s+
means a number a little larger than
s
.

Regarding the function space, first
L_{+}^{p}=L^{p}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})
, where
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}=\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}.
When
p=2
,

(a,b)_{L_{+}^{2}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}a(x,y)b(x,y)dxdy,

denotes the standard
L^{2}
inner product. If
X
is a Banach space and
I
is a subinterval of
\mathbb{R}
, then
L^{q}(I,X)
means that the map
t\mapsto\|f(t)\|_{X}
belongs to
L^{q}(I)
. In particular, we use
L^{p}_{T}(L^{q}_{\mathrm{h}}(L^{r}_{\mathrm{v}}))
to denote
L^{p}([0,T];L^{q}(\mathbb{R}_{x};L^{r}(\mathbb{R}_{y}^{+})))
.

The anisotropic Sobolev space is defined as follows, let
s\in\mathbb{R},k\in\mathbb{N}
, then

\|f\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{s}|\hat{f}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\|f\|_{H^{s,k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}\sum\limits_{0\leq l\leq k}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{s}|\partial_{y}^{l}\hat{f}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In particular, the weighted space is defined as follows:

\|f\|_{H^{s,k}_{\Psi}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}\sum\limits_{0\leq l\leq k}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\Psi(t,y)}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{s}|\partial_{y}^{l}\hat{f}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

here
\Psi(t,y)=\frac{y^{2}}{8\langle t\rangle}.

The inner product of the inner product space
H_{\Psi}^{\sigma,0}
is defined as follows:

(f,g)_{H_{\Psi}^{\sigma,0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}\mathrm{Re}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\Psi}[D_{x}]^{\sigma}f\overline{[D_{x}]^{\sigma}g}dxdy.

In addition, to keep the notation simple, for the Banach space
X
, we specify

\|\partial_{y}^{k}(f,g)\|_{X}^{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}\|\partial_{y}^{k}f\|_{X}^{m}+\|\partial_{y}^{k}g\|_{X}^{m}.

2.2.
Main Result

Using (
1
), we can rewrite (
1
) in the following form

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\left(\partial_{t}+u\partial_{x}+v\partial_{y}-\partial_{y}^{2}\right)u=\dot{\theta}P,\\
\left(\partial_{t}+u\partial_{x}+v\partial_{y}-\kappa\partial_{y}^{2}\right)b=\dot{\theta}N,\\
\left(\partial_{t}+u\partial_{x}+v\partial_{y}-\kappa\partial_{y}^{2}\right)h=b\partial_{x}v-h\partial_{x}u,\end{array}\right.

where
\theta(t)
is determined by (
3.14
),
P
and
N
are as follows:

(2.4)

\displaystyle P=\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}\left(b\partial_{x}+h\partial_{y}\right)b,~N=\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}\left(b\partial_{x}+h\partial_{y}\right)u.

Remark 2.1
.

In contrast to
[
19
]
, we isolate
\dot{\theta}(t)
in the definition of
(P,N)
, which is crucial for this paper, and the role of
\dot{\theta}
can be seen in the remark
3.2
.

Since (
1
), we can know that there exist stream functions
(\psi,\tilde{\psi})
such that
(u,b)=\partial_{y}(\psi,\tilde{\psi})
and
(v,h)=-\partial_{x}(\psi,\tilde{\psi})
hold and have boundary values

(\psi,\tilde{\psi})|_{y=0}=(\psi,\tilde{\psi})|_{y\rightarrow+\infty}=(0,0).

The detailed derivation can be found in
[
15
]
.

Integrating the equation (
2.2
) with respect to the
y
variable over
[y,+\infty)
, we can obtain the stream function satisfying the following equation

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\psi+u\partial_{x}\psi+2\int_{y}^{\infty}\left(\partial_{y}u\partial_{x}\psi\right)dy^{\prime}-\partial_{y}^{2}\psi+\int_{y}^{\infty}\dot{\theta}Pdy^{\prime}=0,\\
\partial_{t}\tilde{\psi}+u\partial_{x}\tilde{\psi}+\int_{y}^{\infty}\left(\partial_{y}u\partial_{x}\tilde{\psi}+\partial_{y}b\partial_{x}\psi\right)dy^{\prime}-\kappa\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{\psi}+\int_{y}^{\infty}\dot{\theta}Ndy^{\prime}=0,\\
\left.(\psi,\tilde{\psi})\right|_{y=0}=\left.(\psi,\tilde{\psi})\right|_{y\rightarrow+\infty}=(0,0),\\
\left.(\psi,\tilde{\psi})\right|_{t=0}=(\psi_{0},\tilde{\psi}_{0})=(-\int_{y}^{\infty}u_{0}dz,-\int_{y}^{\infty}b_{0}dz).\end{array}\right.

In order to obtain faster decay estimates for the solution of the equation (
2.2
), we need to control the Gevrey radius of the solution, so we need to introduce the following “good function” (see
[
15
,
24
,
26
]
):

(2.5)

\displaystyle G=u+\frac{y}{2\langle t\rangle}\psi,~~\tilde{G}=b+\frac{y}{2\kappa\langle t\rangle}\tilde{\psi}.

By direct calculation, we can obtain that
(G,\tilde{G})
satisfies the following equation:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}G-\partial_{y}^{2}G+\langle t\rangle^{-1}G+u\partial_{x}G+v\partial_{y}G-\frac{1}{2\langle t\rangle}v\partial_{y}(y\psi)\\
~~+\frac{y}{\langle t\rangle}\int_{y}^{\infty}\left(\partial_{y}u\partial_{x}\psi\right)dy^{\prime}-\dot{\theta}P+\frac{y}{2\langle t\rangle}\int_{y}^{\infty}\dot{\theta}Pdy^{\prime}=0,\\
\partial_{t}\tilde{G}-\kappa\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{G}+\langle t\rangle^{-1}\tilde{G}+u\partial_{x}\tilde{G}+v\partial_{y}\tilde{G}-\frac{1}{2\kappa\langle t\rangle}v\partial_{y}(y\tilde{\psi})\\
~~+\frac{y}{2\kappa\langle t\rangle}\int_{y}^{\infty}\left(\partial_{y}u\partial_{x}\tilde{\psi}+\partial_{y}b\partial_{x}\psi\right)dy^{\prime}-\dot{\theta}N+\frac{y}{2\kappa\langle t\rangle}\int_{y}^{\infty}\dot{\theta}Ndy^{\prime}=0,\\
\left.(G,\tilde{G})\right|_{y=0}=\lim_{y\rightarrow+\infty}(G,\tilde{G})=(0,0),\\
\left.(G,\tilde{G})\right|_{t=0}=(G_{0},\tilde{G}_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}(u_{0}+\frac{y}{2}\psi_{0},b_{0}+\frac{y}{2\kappa}\tilde{\psi}_{0}).\end{array}\right.

Let
[\xi]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}(1+\xi^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
,
D_{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}\frac{1}{i}\partial_{x}
, then
[D_{x}]^{s}
denotes the differential operator whose corresponding Fourier product is
(1+|\xi|^{2})^{\frac{s}{2}}
. Let
f_{\Phi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}e^{\Phi(t,D_{x})}f
, where

(2.12)

\displaystyle\Phi(t,D_{x})=\delta(t)[D_{x}]^{\frac{2}{3}},~\delta(t)=\delta-\lambda\theta(t).

Our main theorem is stated as follows:

Theorem 2.1
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,2)
be a given constant, and
\eta
is also a fixed constant satisfying
0<\eta<l_{\kappa}
, where
l_{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa(2-\kappa)}{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].
The initial values satisfy
u_{0}|_{y=0}=b_{0}|_{y=0}=0,\int_{0}^{\infty}u_{0}dy=\int_{0}^{\infty}b_{0}dy=0.

Define
E(t)
and
D(t)
as follows:

(2.13)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} E(t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}&\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\zeta,\tilde{\zeta})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\\
&+\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(P_{\Phi},N_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{20}{3},0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{1+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(G_{\Phi},\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{4,0}}\\
&+\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{2+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}\\
&+\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{3}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{3}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{2,0}},\end{aligned}

and

(2.14)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} D(t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}&\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}u_{\Phi},\partial_{y}b_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}\zeta,\partial_{y}\tilde{\zeta})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\\
&+\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}P_{\Phi},\partial_{y}N_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{20}{3},0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{1+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{4,0}}\\
&+\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{2+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{3}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{3}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}\\
&+\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{4}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{4}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{2,0}}.\end{aligned}

where
\mathcal{U}
is defined by (
3.1
). If the initial data satisfy

(2.15)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|(u_{\Phi}(0),b_{\Phi}(0))\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+E(0)\leq\epsilon^{2}.\end{aligned}

Then there exist
\epsilon_{0},\lambda_{0}>0
such that when
0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{0},\lambda>\lambda_{0}
, we have

1)
\theta(t)
in (
2.12
) satisfies
\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\theta(t)\leq\frac{\delta}{4\lambda}
.

2)
The system (
1
)-(
1
) has a unique global solution
(u,b)
, satisfying

(2.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} E(t)+\frac{79}{100}\eta\int_{0}^{t}D(s)ds\leq C\epsilon^{2}\end{aligned}

for any
t\in[0,\infty)
.

2.3.
Littlewood-Paley theory

For the convenience of the reader, we give some basics of Littlewood-Paley theory in this subsection. First we have (see
[
2
]
),

\displaystyle S_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}f=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\chi(2^{-k}|\xi|)\hat{f})~~and~~\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}f=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi(2^{-k}|\xi|)\hat{f})~~if~~k\geq~0,\\
S_{0}^{\mathrm{h}}f~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~if~~k=~-1,\\
0~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~if~~k\leq-2.\end{array}\right.

In this paper,
\mathcal{F}f
and
\hat{f}
always denote the Fourier transform of the distribution
f
with respect to
x
variable. On the other hand,
\chi
and
\varphi
denote smooth functions with compact support, the support of which is shown below:

\text{Supp}~\varphi\subset\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}|\frac{3}{4}\leq|\xi|\leq\frac{8}{3}\}~~and~~\forall\xi\neq 0,~\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\varphi(2^{-k}\xi)=1,

\text{Supp}~\chi\subset\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}|~|\xi|\leq\frac{4}{3}\}~~and~~\chi(\xi)+\sum_{k\geq 0}\varphi(2^{-k}\xi)=1.

Next, in order to estimate the product of the two distributions, we need to use the Bony’s decomposition of the horizontal variable
x
frequently (see
[
3
]
):

(2.20)

\displaystyle fg=T^{\mathrm{h}}_{f}g+T^{\mathrm{h}}_{g}f+R^{\mathrm{h}}(f,g),

where
T^{\mathrm{h}}_{f}g=\sum\limits_{k}S_{k-1}^{\mathrm{h}}f\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}g,~R^{\mathrm{h}}(f,g)=\sum\limits_{|k-k^{\prime}|\leq 1}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}f\Delta_{k^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{h}}g.

2.4.
Para-product estimates

Next we always assume that
t<T^{*}
, where
T^{*}
is defined as follows:

(2.21)

\displaystyle T^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}\sup\left\{t>0,\theta(t)<\frac{\delta}{2\lambda}\right\}.

By (
2.12
), we know that when
t<T^{*}
, we have
\delta(t)\geq\frac{\delta}{2}.
And since
|([\xi])^{\prime}|=|\dfrac{\xi}{(1+\xi^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}|<1
, so

[\xi]=[\xi-\eta+\eta]\leq[\xi-\eta]+|\eta|\leq[\xi-\eta]+[\eta].

Then

[\xi]^{\frac{2}{3}}\leq|[\xi]-[\eta]|^{\frac{2}{3}}+[\eta]^{\frac{2}{3}}\leq[\xi-\eta]^{\frac{2}{3}}+[\eta]^{\frac{2}{3}},

thus we can obtain the convex inequality for
\Phi(t,\xi)=\delta(t)[\xi]^{\frac{2}{3}}
:

(2.22)

\displaystyle\Phi(t,\xi)\leq\Phi(t,\xi-\eta)+\Phi(t,\eta),

holds for any
\xi,\eta\in\mathbb{R}
. Using the same proof as in
[
2
]
(for the case
\Phi(t,\xi)=0
), combined with convexity, we can obtain the following two lemmas (see also
[
26
]
):

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
, and
(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{f})^{*}
denotes the adjoint operator of
T^{\mathrm{h}}_{f}
. If
\sigma>\frac{1}{2},
then

\left\|\left(T_{f}^{\mathrm{h}}g\right)_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}\leq C\left\|f_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma}}\left\|g_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}},

\left\|\left(\left(T_{f}^{\mathrm{h}}\right)^{*}g\right)_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}\leq C\left\|f_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma}}\left\|g_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}.

If
s_{1}+s_{2}>s+\frac{1}{2}>0,
we also have

\left\|\left(R^{\mathrm{h}}(f,g)\right)_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}\leq C\left\|f_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s_{1}}}\left\|g_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s_{2}}}.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
s>0,\sigma>\frac{3}{2}
, then we have

\left\|\left(\left(T_{a}^{\mathrm{h}}T_{b}^{\mathrm{h}}-T_{ab}^{\mathrm{h}}\right)f\right)_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}\leq C\left\|a_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma}}\left\|b_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma}}\left\|f_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s-1}},

\left\|\left(\left[\left[D_{x}\right]^{s};T_{a}^{\mathrm{h}}\right]f\right)_{\Phi}\right\|_{L_{\mathrm{h}}^{2}}\leq C\left\|a_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma}}\left\|f_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s-1}},

\left\|\left(\left(T_{a}^{\mathrm{h}}-\left(T_{a}^{\mathrm{h}}\right)^{*}\right)f\right)_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}\leq C\left\|a_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma}}\left\|f_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s-1}},

\left\|\left(\left[T_{a}^{\mathrm{h}};T_{b}^{\mathrm{h}}\right]f\right)_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}\leq C\left\|a_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma}}\left\|b_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma}}\left\|f_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s-1}},

where
[a;b]
denotes the commutator.

Compared to the Prandtl equation, (
2.2
) adds the effect of the magnetic field. In order to deal with the difficulties caused by the Lorentz force, we need the following lemma:

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
\sigma>\frac{3}{2},s_{1},s_{2}>0
, then

\left|\left([D_{x}]^{s_{1}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}\partial_{x}f,[D_{x}]^{s_{2}}g\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}+\left([D_{x}]^{s_{2}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}\partial_{x}g,[D_{x}]^{s_{1}}f\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}\right|\leq C\|a\|_{H^{\sigma}_{\mathrm{h}}}\|f\|_{H^{s_{1}}_{\mathrm{h}}}\|g\|_{H^{s_{2}}_{\mathrm{h}}}.

Proof.

\displaystyle~~~\left([D_{x}]^{s_{1}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}\partial_{x}f,[D_{x}]^{s_{2}}g\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}+\left([D_{x}]^{s_{2}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}\partial_{x}g,[D_{x}]^{s_{1}}f\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\left[[D_{x}]^{s_{1}};T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}\right]\partial_{x}f,[D_{x}]^{s_{2}}g\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}+\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}[D_{x}]^{s_{1}}\partial_{x}f,[D_{x}]^{s_{2}}g\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}+\left([D_{x}]^{s_{2}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}\partial_{x}g,[D_{x}]^{s_{1}}f\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\left[[D_{x}]^{s_{1}};T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}\right]\partial_{x}f,[D_{x}]^{s_{2}}g\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}+\left((T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}-(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a})^{*})[D_{x}]^{s_{1}}\partial_{x}f,[D_{x}]^{s_{2}}g\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}

\displaystyle~~~~+\left(\left[[D_{x}]^{s_{2}};T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}\right]\partial_{x}g,[D_{x}]^{s_{1}}f\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}+\left((T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a})^{*}[D_{x}]^{s_{1}}\partial_{x}f,[D_{x}]^{s_{2}}g\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}

\displaystyle~~~~+\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}[D_{x}]^{s_{2}}\partial_{x}g,[D_{x}]^{s_{1}}f\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\left[[D_{x}]^{s_{1}};T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}\right]\partial_{x}f,[D_{x}]^{s_{2}}g\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}+\left((T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}-(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a})^{*})[D_{x}]^{s_{1}}\partial_{x}f,[D_{x}]^{s_{2}}g\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}

\displaystyle~~~~+\left(\left[[D_{x}]^{s_{2}};T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}\right]\partial_{x}g,[D_{x}]^{s_{1}}f\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}+\left(\left[T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a};\partial_{x}\right][D_{x}]^{s_{2}}g,[D_{x}]^{s_{1}}f\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}.

Using the H
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
lder’s inequality and the lemma
2.3
, we can complete the proof of the lemma
2.4
.
∎

Remark 2.2
.

In particular, if we take
f=g,s_{1}=s_{2}=s>0
in lemma
2.4
, then we have

\left|\left([D_{x}]^{s}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}\partial_{x}f,[D_{x}]^{s}f\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}\right|\leq C\|a\|_{H^{\sigma}_{\mathrm{h}}}\|f\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{\mathrm{h}}}.

This inequality is used in
[
26
]
.

Next, we give two estimates of the commutator of
e^{\Phi}
and
T^{\mathrm{h}}_{a}
. The proof of
\Phi(t,\xi)=\delta(t)[\xi]^{\frac{1}{2}}
is given in
[
26
]
, and the result when
\Phi(t,\xi)=\delta(t)[\xi]^{\frac{2}{3}}
can be obtained with a slight modification of the proof.

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
\Phi(t,\xi)=\delta(t)[\xi]^{\frac{2}{3}}
,
\sigma>\frac{3}{2},s\in\mathbb{R}
. Then we have

\left\|\left(T_{a}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{x}f\right)_{\Phi}-T_{a}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{x}f_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}\leq C\delta(t)\left\|a_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma}}\left\|f_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s+\frac{2}{3}}}.

Lemma 2.6
.

Let
\Phi(t,\xi)=\delta(t)[\xi]^{\frac{2}{3}},Q(\xi)=\xi(1+\xi^{2})^{-\frac{2}{3}}
,

\sigma>\frac{5}{2},s\in\mathbb{R}.
If
0<\delta(t)\leq L
, then the following inequality holds,

\left\|\left(T_{a}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{x}f\right)_{\Phi}-T_{a}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{x}f_{\Phi}-\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T_{D_{x}a}^{\mathrm{h}}Q(D_{x})\partial_{x}f_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}\leq C_{L}\left\|a_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma}}\left\|f_{\Phi}\right\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s+\frac{1}{3}}}.

In order to obtain the decay estimates of the solution, we need to use the following two lemmas. The first is a Poincar
\mathrm{\acute{e}}
type inequality, see
[
10
,
26
]
.

Lemma 2.7
.

Let
\Psi=\frac{y^{2}}{8\langle t\rangle},d
be a non-negative integer. The function
u
is smooth enough on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
and decays rapidly to
0
as
y
tends to
+\infty
. At this point we have the following two inequalities,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left|\partial_{y}u(X,y)\right|^{2}e^{2\Psi}dXdy\geq\frac{1}{2\langle t\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u(X,y)|^{2}e^{2\Psi}dXdy,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left|\partial_{y}u(X,y)\right|^{2}e^{2\Psi}dXdy

\displaystyle\geq\frac{s}{2\langle t\rangle}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u(X,y)|^{2}e^{2\Psi}dXdy

\displaystyle+\frac{s(1-s)}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left|\frac{y}{\langle t\rangle}u(X,y)\right|^{2}e^{2\Psi}dXdy,

where
s>0.

The following lemma shows that the decay rate of the solution
(u,b)
of the equation (
2.2
) and the stream function
(\psi,\tilde{\psi})
can be controlled by the decay rate of the “good function”
(G,\tilde{G})
.

Lemma 2.8
.

Let
(u,b)
be the sufficiently smooth solution of the equation (
2.2
) on
[0,T]
,
(\psi,\tilde{\psi})
be the corresponding stream function.
(G,\tilde{G})
is defined by (
2.5
), and the weight function is
\Psi(t,y)=\frac{y^{2}}{8\langle t\rangle}
. If
\kappa\in(0,2)
, then for any
\gamma\in(0,1),t\leq T,
we have the following inequalities hold,

(2.23)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\|e^{\gamma\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}u_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}}&\lesssim\left\|e^{\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}G_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}},\\
\left\|e^{\gamma\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}u_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}}&\lesssim\left\|e^{\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}G_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}},\\
\left\|e^{\gamma\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}^{2}u_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}}&\lesssim\left\|e^{\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}},\\
\left\|e^{\gamma\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}^{3}u_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}}&\lesssim\left\|e^{\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}^{3}G_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}},\\
\left\|e^{\gamma\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}^{2}u_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{\infty}(L_{\mathrm{h}}^{2})}&\lesssim\left\|e^{\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{\infty}(L_{\mathrm{h}}^{2})},\\
\langle t\rangle^{-1}\left\|e^{\gamma\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}(y\psi)_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}}&+\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\|e^{\gamma\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}^{2}(y\psi)_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}}\\
+\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{3}{4}}\left\|e^{\gamma\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}(y\psi)_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{\infty}\left(L_{\mathrm{h}}^{2}\right)}+\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{4}}&\left\|e^{\gamma\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}^{2}(y\psi)_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{\infty}\left(L_{\mathrm{h}}^{2}\right)}\lesssim\left\|e^{\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}G_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}},\\
\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\|e^{\gamma\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}^{3}(y\psi)_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}}&\lesssim\left\|e^{\Psi}\Delta_{k}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi}(t)\right\|_{L_{+}^{2}}.\end{aligned}

The above inequalities also hold if we replace
(u,\psi,G)
with
(b,\tilde{\psi},\tilde{G})
.

Except for
\eqref{henyouyong}_{4}
which is not proved, the proof of the rest of the inequalities is given in
[
15
]
and
[
26
]
. The proof of
\eqref{henyouyong}_{4}
is the same as that of
\eqref{henyouyong}_{3}
, so for the sake of brevity, we do not give the proof here.

Remark 2.3
.

Notice that each term of our lemma
2.8
contains the operator
\Phi

(
compared to
[
26
]
with some differences
)
, this is because we have to do the estimates of
\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi}
and
\partial_{y}^{3}G_{\Phi}
to close the a priori estimates, while in
[
26
]
only the estimates of
\partial_{y}^{2}G
and
\partial_{y}^{3}G
. See (
3.15
) the definition of
T_{*}
for detail.

3.
Sketch of The Proof to Theorem
2.1

In this section, we give the outline of the proof of theorem
2.1
. First we rewrite the form of the equation (
2.2
).

3.1.
New formulation of the equation

To make Gevrey norm estimates for the solution of the equation (
2.2
), we use the operator
e^{\Phi(t,D_{x})}
to act on the equation (
2.2
) and do the Bony’s decomposition of all nonlinear terms using (
2.20
). In order to control the higher order terms by using the lemma
2.4
, lemma
2.5
and lemma
2.6
, we write the equation in the following form:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{L}u_{\Phi}=-T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}u}v_{\Phi}-\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}D_{x}u}Q(D_{x})v_{\Phi}-A+\dot{\theta}P_{\Phi}\\
\mathcal{L_{\kappa}}b_{\Phi}=-T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b}v_{\Phi}-\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}D_{x}b}Q(D_{x})v_{\Phi}-B+\dot{\theta}N_{\Phi}\\
\partial_{x}u_{\Phi}+\partial_{y}v_{\Phi}=\partial_{x}b_{\Phi}+\partial_{y}h_{\Phi}=0,\\
(u_{\Phi},v_{\Phi},b_{\Phi},h_{\Phi})|_{y=0}=(0,0,0,0),~(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})|_{y\rightarrow+\infty}=(0,0),\\
(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})|_{t=0}=(e^{\delta[D_{x}]^{\frac{2}{3}}}u_{0},e^{\delta[D_{x}]^{\frac{2}{3}}}b_{0}).\end{array}\right.

where the operator
\mathcal{L_{\kappa}}
is defined as follows:

(3.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L_{\kappa}}=\partial_{t}+\lambda\dot{\theta}(t)[D_{x}]^{\frac{2}{3}}+T^{\mathrm{h}}_{u}\partial_{x}+T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}\partial_{y}+\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{D_{x}u}Q(D_{x})\partial_{x}-\kappa\partial_{y}^{2},

In particular, it is denoted by
\mathcal{L}
when
\kappa=1
. The definitions of
A
and
B
are as follows:

(3.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}A=&\left(T_{u}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{x}u\right)_{\Phi}-T_{u}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{x}u_{\Phi}-\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T_{D_{x}u}^{\mathrm{h}}Q(D_{x})\partial_{x}u_{\Phi}\\
&+\left(T_{\partial_{y}u}^{\mathrm{h}}v\right)_{\Phi}-T_{\partial_{y}u}^{\mathrm{h}}v_{\Phi}-\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T_{\partial_{y}D_{x}u}^{\mathrm{h}}Q(D_{x})v_{\Phi}+\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}\partial_{y}u\right)_{\Phi}-T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}\partial_{y}u_{\Phi}\\
&+\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{x}u}u+R^{\mathrm{h}}(u,\partial_{x}u)+R^{\mathrm{h}}(v,\partial_{y}u)\right)_{\Phi},\end{split}

(3.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}B=&\left(T_{u}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{x}b\right)_{\Phi}-T_{u}^{\mathrm{h}}\partial_{x}b_{\Phi}-\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T_{D_{x}u}^{\mathrm{h}}Q(D_{x})\partial_{x}b_{\Phi}\\
&+\left(T_{\partial_{y}b}^{\mathrm{h}}v\right)_{\Phi}-T_{\partial_{y}b}^{\mathrm{h}}v_{\Phi}-\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T_{\partial_{y}D_{x}b}^{\mathrm{h}}Q(D_{x})v_{\Phi}+\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}\partial_{y}b\right)_{\Phi}-T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}\partial_{y}b_{\Phi}\\
&+\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{x}b}u+R^{\mathrm{h}}(u,\partial_{x}b)+R^{\mathrm{h}}(v,\partial_{y}b)\right)_{\Phi}.\end{split}

Inspired by
[
6
,
19
,
26
]
, in order to deal with the difficult term
v_{\Phi}
, let
\mathcal{U}
be the solution of the following initial boundary value problem,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{L}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz=-\dot{\theta}(t)v_{\Phi},\\
\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}|_{y=0}=\lim\limits_{y\rightarrow+\infty}\mathcal{U}=0,\\
\mathcal{U}|_{t=0}=0.\end{array}\right.

Here the existence of
\mathcal{U}
can be guaranteed by the proposition
3.1
and the proposition
3.2
.

Remark 3.1
.

The reason for using
\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz
(instead of
\int_{0}^{y}\mathcal{U}dz
) in (
3.1
) is to ensure that the solution
\mathcal{U}
decays quickly to
0
as
y
tends to
+\infty
.

Remark 3.2
.

In (
3.1
),
v_{\Phi}
is preceded by a
\dot{\theta}(t),
which is intended to make Gevrey norm estimates of
\mathcal{U}
along with estimates of its Gevrey radius. This idea goes back to Chemin
[
5
]
, who used this method for the classical Navier-stokes equations. Ping Zhang and others
[
26
]
used this method to obtain the global small solution of the two-dimensional Prandtl equation in the optimal Gevrey space.

By applying
\partial_{y}
to (
3.1
), we obtain the following equation

(3.12)

\displaystyle\dot{\theta}\partial_{x}u_{\Phi}=-\mathcal{L}\mathcal{U}+T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}u}\partial_{x}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz-T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}v}\mathcal{U}+\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}D_{x}u}Q(D_{x})\partial_{x}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz.

In addition to
\mathcal{U}
, we introduce the following two quantities:

(3.13)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\zeta=u_{\Phi}-\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}u}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz-\frac{2\delta(t)}{3\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}D_{x}u}Q(D_{x})\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz,\\
\tilde{\zeta}=b_{\Phi}-\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz-\frac{2\delta(t)}{3\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}D_{x}b}Q(D_{x})\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz.\end{split}

Following the pioneering discovery in
[
26
]
, we define
\theta
by the following equation

(3.14)

\displaystyle\dot{\theta}(t)=\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{-\alpha},~~\theta(0)=0.

where
\epsilon>0
is sufficiently small and
\alpha>1
is a constant that will be fixed in the section
3.3
.

Notice that
(G,\tilde{G})
and
T^{*}
are determined by (
2.5
) and (
2.21
), respectively. Let
\gamma_{0}\in(1,1+l_{\kappa})
and
C
is a sufficiently large constant, then finally define
T_{*}
:

(3.15)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} T_{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}&\sup\left\{t<T^{*},\left\|(G_{\Phi}(t),\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{H_{\Psi}^{4,0}}+\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}\right.\\
&\left.+\langle t\rangle\left\|(\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}+\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\left\|(\partial_{y}^{3}G_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}^{3}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{H_{\Psi}^{2,0}}\leq C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-\gamma_{0}}\right\}.\end{aligned}

3.2.
The a priori estimates

In this subsection, we list all the a priori estimates needed to prove theorem
2.1
.

To obtain the Gevrey norm estimates for
(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})
, we first derive a priori estimates for the auxiliary functions
\mathcal{U}
and
(\zeta,\tilde{\zeta})
in section
4
and section
5
.

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,2)
be a given constant. Let
\mathcal{U}
be a sufficiently smooth solution of the equation (
3.1
) on
[0,T_{*}]
and
\mathcal{U}
decays rapidly to
0
as
y
tends to
+\infty
. Then there exist
\epsilon_{1}
and
\lambda_{1}
such that when
\epsilon<\epsilon_{1},\lambda>\lambda_{1}
and
\alpha\leq\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4}
, the following inequality holds for any
t<T_{*}
and sufficiently small
\eta>0
:

(3.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\eta\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds\\
+&\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}(s)\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds\leq\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}(s)\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\zeta(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}ds,\end{aligned}

where
l_{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa(2-\kappa)}{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,2)
be a given constant. Let
(\zeta,\tilde{\zeta})
be defined by (
3.13
). Then there exist
\epsilon_{2}
and
\lambda_{2}
such that when
\epsilon<\epsilon_{2},\lambda>\lambda_{2}
and
\alpha\leq\min\{\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4},\frac{2}{3}\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{8}\}
, for any
t<T_{*}
and sufficiently small
\eta>0
, the following inequality holds:

(3.17)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\zeta(t),\tilde{\zeta}(t))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\eta\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}\zeta,\partial_{y}\tilde{\zeta})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds\\
&+\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\zeta,\tilde{\zeta})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}ds\\
\leq&\|(u_{\Phi}(0),b_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\frac{2}{25}\eta\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds\\
&+\frac{1}{5}\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\left(\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(P_{\Phi},N_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\right)ds,\end{aligned}

where
l_{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa(2-\kappa)}{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].

Using the estimates of the auxiliary functions
\mathcal{U}
and
(\zeta,\tilde{\zeta})
, in section
6
we give the a priori estimates of
(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,2)
be a given constant. Let
(u,b)
be a sufficiently smooth solution of the equation (
2.2
) on
[0,T_{*}]
and
(u,b)
decays rapidly to
0
as
y
tends to
+\infty
. Then there exist
\epsilon_{2}
and
\lambda_{2}
such that when
\epsilon<\epsilon_{2},\lambda>\lambda_{2}
and
\alpha\leq\min\{\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4},\frac{2}{3}\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{8}\}
, for any
t<T_{*}
and sufficiently small
\eta>0
, the following inequality holds:

(3.18)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(u_{\Phi}(t),b_{\Phi}(t))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds\\
&+\eta\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}u_{\Phi},\partial_{y}b_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds\\
\leq&2\|(u_{\Phi}(0),b_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\frac{1}{5}\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(P_{\Phi},N_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds,\end{aligned}

where
l_{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa(2-\kappa)}{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].

In section
7
, we will deal with the difficulty term
(P_{\Phi},N_{\Phi})
caused by the magnetic field.

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,2)
be a given constant. Let
(P,N)
be defined by (
2.4
). Then there exist
\epsilon_{3}
and
\lambda_{2}
such that when
\epsilon<\epsilon_{3},\lambda>\lambda_{2}
and
\alpha\leq\min\{\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4},\frac{2}{3}\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{8}\}
, for any
t<T_{*}
and sufficiently small
\eta>0
, the following inequality holds:

(3.19)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(P_{\Phi}(t),N_{\Phi}(t))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{20}{3},0}}+\eta\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}P_{\Phi},\partial_{y}N_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{20}{3},0}}ds\\
&+\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(P_{\Phi},N_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds\\
\leq&\|(P_{\Phi}(0),N_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{20}{3},0}}+\frac{1}{10}\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\left(\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\right)ds\\
&+\frac{13}{100}\eta\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}\zeta,\partial_{y}\tilde{\zeta})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\right)ds,\end{aligned}

where
l_{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa(2-\kappa)}{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].

From section
8
to section
11
, we will give the Gevrey norm estimates for
(G_{\Phi},\tilde{G}_{\Phi})
and
(\partial_{y}^{i}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{i}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}),

i=1,2,3
respectively. These estimates are very important to control the Gevrey radius and thus obtain the global solution.

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,2)
be a given constant. Let
(G,\tilde{G})
be defined by (
2.5
). Then there exist
\epsilon_{3}
and
\lambda_{2}
such that when
\epsilon<\epsilon_{3},\lambda>\lambda_{2}
and
\alpha\leq\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{36}
, for any
t<T_{*}
and sufficiently small
\eta>0
the following inequality holds:

(3.20)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\langle t\rangle^{1+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(G_{\Phi}(t),\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{4,0}}+\eta\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{1+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{4,0}}ds\\
&+\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}(s)\|\langle s\rangle^{1+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(G_{\Phi},\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{13}{3},0}}ds\\
\leq&\|(G_{\Phi}(0),\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{4,0}}+\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\sqrt{a}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds,\end{aligned}

where
l_{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa(2-\kappa)}{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,2)
be a given constant. Let
(G,\tilde{G})
be defined by (
2.5
). Then there exist
\epsilon_{3}
and
\lambda_{2}
such that when
\epsilon<\epsilon_{3},\lambda>\lambda_{2}
and
\alpha\leq\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{36}
, for any
t<T_{*}
and sufficiently small
\eta>0
the following inequality holds:

(3.21)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}+\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\langle s\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{10}{3},0}}ds\\
&+(4l_{\kappa}-\frac{7}{25}\eta)\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}ds\\
\leq&C\eta^{-1}\left(\|(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi}(0),\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}+\|(G_{\Phi}(0),\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{4,0}}\right)\\
&+C\eta^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\sqrt{a}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds,\end{aligned}

where
l_{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa(2-\kappa)}{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].

The estimates of
(\partial_{y}^{i}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{i}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}),i=2,3
are crucial to deal with the difficulties posed by the magnetic field.

Proposition 3.7
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,2)
be a given constant. Let
(G,\tilde{G})
be defined by (
2.5
). Then there exist
\epsilon_{3}
and
\lambda_{2}
such that when
\epsilon<\epsilon_{3},\lambda>\lambda_{2}
and
\alpha\leq\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{36}
, for any
t<T_{*}
and sufficiently small
\eta>0
the following inequality holds:

(3.22)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\langle t\rangle^{2+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}+(4l_{\kappa}-\frac{13}{100}\eta)\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{2+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{3}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{3}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}ds\\
&+\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\langle s\rangle^{2+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{10}{3},0}}ds\\
\leq&\dfrac{C}{\eta(4l_{\kappa}-\frac{7}{25}\eta)}\left(\|(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi}(0),\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}+\|(G_{\Phi}(0),\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{4,0}}+\|(\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi}(0),\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}\right)\\
&+\dfrac{C}{\eta(4l_{\kappa}-\frac{7}{25}\eta)}\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\sqrt{a}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds,\end{aligned}

where
l_{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa(2-\kappa)}{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].

Proposition 3.8
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,2)
be a given constant. Let
(G,\tilde{G})
be defined by (
2.5
). Then there exist
\epsilon_{3}
and
\lambda_{2}
such that when
\epsilon<\epsilon_{3},\lambda>\lambda_{2}
and
\alpha\leq\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{36}
, for any
t<T_{*}
and sufficiently small
\eta>0
the following inequality holds:

(3.23)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{3}G_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}^{3}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{2,0}}+(4l_{\kappa}-\frac{1}{25}\eta)\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{4}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{4}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{2,0}}ds\\
&+\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\langle s\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{3}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{3}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{7}{3},0}}ds\\
\leq&\dfrac{C}{\eta(4l_{\kappa}-\frac{7}{25}\eta)(4l_{\kappa}-\frac{13}{100}\eta)}\left(\|(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi}(0),\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}+\|(G_{\Phi}(0),\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{4,0}}\right.\\
&\left.+\|(\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi}(0),\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}+\|(\partial_{y}^{3}G_{\Phi}(0),\partial_{y}^{3}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{2,0}}\right)\\
&+\dfrac{C}{\eta(4l_{\kappa}-\frac{7}{25}\eta)(4l_{\kappa}-\frac{13}{100}\eta)}\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\sqrt{a}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds,\end{aligned}

where
l_{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa(2-\kappa)}{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].

3.3.
Proof of theorem
2.1

Using the previous a priori estimates, we will give the proof of theorem
2.1
in this section.

Proof of Theorem
2.1
:

First of all, the existence of the local solution of the equation (
1
)-(
1
) can be found in
[
19
]
. Although the boundary condition in
[
19
]
is
\partial_{y}b|_{y=0}=0
, it is easy to verify that the conclusion also holds for the boundary condition
b|_{y=0}=0
, since no additional boundary term is generated when we use integrating by parts.

Therefore it may be assumed that the equation (
1
)-(
1
) has a solution on
[0,\mathfrak{T}]
. If
\mathfrak{T}<\infty,
then choose
\eta\in(0,l_{\kappa})
and let
\gamma_{0}
in (
3.15
) be taken as
\gamma_{0}=1+l_{\kappa}-\eta
. At this point there is obvious

\min\{\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{36},\frac{2}{3}\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{8}\}=\frac{9}{8}+\frac{1}{2}l_{\kappa}-\frac{1}{2}\eta.

Since (
3.15
), we define
T_{*}^{\eta}
as follows:

(3.24)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} T_{*}^{\eta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}&\sup\left\{t<T^{*},\left\|(G_{\Phi}(t),\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{H_{\Psi}^{4,0}}+\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}\right.\\
&\left.+\langle t\rangle\left\|(\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{H_{\Psi}^{3,0}}+\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\left\|(\partial_{y}^{3}G_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}^{3}\tilde{G}_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{H_{\Psi}^{2,0}}\leq C_{0}\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-1-l_{\kappa}+\eta}\right\},\end{aligned}

where
T^{*}
is defined by (
2.21
), the constant
C_{0}
is to be determined later on.

Let
E(t)
and
D(t)
are defined by (
2.13
) and (
2.14
) respectively. And define
H(t)
as follows:

(3.25)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} H(t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}&\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\zeta,\tilde{\zeta})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}\\
&+\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}(P_{\Phi},N_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{1+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(G_{\Phi},\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{13}{3},0}}\\
&+\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{10}{3},0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{2+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{2}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{2}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{10}{3},0}}\\
&+\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{5}{2}+l_{\kappa}-\eta}(\partial_{y}^{3}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}^{3}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{7}{3},0}}.\end{aligned}

From the choice of
\{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{3},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\}
, it follows that
\epsilon_{1}>\epsilon_{2}>\epsilon_{3}
ÇÒ
\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}
. Thus when
\alpha\leq\frac{9}{8}+\frac{1}{2}l_{\kappa}-\frac{1}{2}\eta,\epsilon<\epsilon_{3}
and
\lambda>\lambda_{2}
, combining
(
3.16
)-(
3.23
) we can obtain

(3.26)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &E(t)+\frac{79}{100}\eta\int_{0}^{t}D(s)ds+\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}H(s)ds\\
\leq&C_{\eta}\left(\|(u_{\Phi}(0),b_{\Phi}(0))\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+E(0)\right)+(1+C_{\eta}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda)\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}H(s)ds\end{aligned}

Let
\lambda_{0}=\max\{2+2C_{\eta},\lambda_{2}\}
. Then when
\lambda>\lambda_{3}
, using
(
2.15
) and (
3.26
), we can get

(3.27)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} E(t)+\frac{79}{100}\eta\int_{0}^{t}D(s)ds\leq C_{\eta}\left(\|(u_{\Phi}(0),b_{\Phi}(0))\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+E(0)\right)\leq C_{\eta}\epsilon^{2}\leq\frac{C_{0}^{2}}{4}\epsilon^{2},\end{aligned}

where we take
C_{0}=2\sqrt{C_{\eta}}
in (
3.24
).

On the other hand, take
\alpha=\frac{9}{8}+\frac{1}{2}l_{\kappa}-\frac{1}{2}\eta,\epsilon<\epsilon_{3},
satisfying all the requirements of proposition
3.1
-proposition
3.8
. At this time, by
(
3.14
) we have

(3.28)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \theta=\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}(s)ds=\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\langle s\rangle^{-\alpha}\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{\delta}{4\lambda}\end{aligned}

holds when
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
, where
\epsilon_{0}=\min\{\epsilon_{3},\dfrac{\delta^{2}}{16C^{2}\lambda^{2}}\}
.

Therefore when
\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
and
\lambda>\lambda_{0}
, (
3.27
) and
(
3.28
) contradict (
2.21
) and (
3.24
). Thus, by a standard continuous argument, it follows that
\mathfrak{T}=\infty.
Then we have completed the proof of theorem
2.1
.

\square

4.
The Gevrey Estimates of
\mathcal{U}

In this section we will give a priori estimates for
\mathcal{U}
. First the smallness and decay of the lower order derivatives of
(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})
are given by the following lemma:

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
(u,b)
be a sufficiently smooth solution of the equation (
2.2
) on
[0,T_{*}]
with
\kappa\in(0,2)
. Then for any
\gamma\in(0,1),t\leq T_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|(u_{\Phi}(t),b_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{H_{\gamma\Psi}^{4,0}}+\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\partial_{y}u_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}b_{\Phi}

\displaystyle(t))\|_{H_{\gamma\Psi}^{3,0}}+\langle t\rangle\left\|(\partial_{y}^{2}u_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}^{2}b_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{H_{\gamma\Psi}^{3,0}}

\displaystyle+\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}\|(\partial_{y}^{3}u_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}^{3}

\displaystyle b_{\Phi}(t))\|_{H_{\gamma\Psi}^{2,0}}\leq C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-\gamma_{0}},

\displaystyle\left\|(u_{\Phi}(t),b_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{v},\gamma\Psi}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{3})}\leq C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-(\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4})}

\displaystyle,~~\left\|(v_{\Phi}(t),h_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{v},\gamma\Psi}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{3})}\leq C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-(\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4})},

\displaystyle\left\|(\partial_{y}u_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}b_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{v},\gamma\Psi}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{3})}\leq C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-(\gamma_{0}+\frac{3}{4})}

\displaystyle,~~\left\|(\partial_{y}^{2}u_{\Phi}(t),\partial_{y}^{2}b_{\Phi}(t))\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{v},\gamma\Psi}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{2})}\leq C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-(\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{4})}.

ÆäÖÐ
\|f\|_{L_{\mathrm{v},\Psi}^{\infty}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{ def }}}{{=}}\|e^{\Psi}f\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{\infty}}
.

This lemma is slightly different from lemma 4.1 in
[
26
]
, but the proof procedure is exactly the same and is not given here.

Remark 4.1
.

Notice that
|f|\leq|e^{\gamma\Psi}f|
, so in fact the conclusion of lemma
4.1
holds for the unweighted case
(\gamma=0)
as well. On the other hand, noting the definition of
(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})
, we can know that the above control also holds for
(u,b)
.

Next, we give a priori estimates for
\mathcal{U}
, and the detailed results are shown below:

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,2)
be a given constant. Let
\mathcal{U}
be a sufficiently smooth solution of the equation (
3.1
) on
[0,T_{*}]
and
\mathcal{U}
decays rapidly to
0
as
y
tends to
+\infty
. Let
a(t)
be a nonnegative and nondecreasing function on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Then when
\alpha\leq\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4}
, for any
t<T_{*}
and sufficiently small
\eta>0
the following inequality holds:

(4.1)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\sqrt{a}&\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}-\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{a^{\prime}}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds+(1-\frac{1}{50}\eta)\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds\\
&+2\left(\lambda-C(1+\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}})\right)\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}(s)\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds\leq\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}(s)\|\sqrt{a}\zeta(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}ds.\end{aligned}

Proof.

Combining (
3.12
) and (
3.13
), we can deduce that

(4.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\mathcal{U}=-\dot{\theta}\partial_{x}\zeta-T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}\partial_{x}u}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz-T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}v}\mathcal{U}-\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}\partial_{x}D_{x}u}Q(D_{x})\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz.

Taking
H_{\Psi}^{7,0}
inner product of
a(t)\mathcal{U}
with (
4.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle a\left(\mathcal{L}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}=

\displaystyle-\dot{\theta}a\left(\partial_{x}\zeta,\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}-a\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}\partial_{x}u}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz,\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}

\displaystyle-a\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}v}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}-a\left(\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}\partial_{x}D_{x}u}Q(D_{x})\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz,\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}.

Using Cauchy’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\dot{\theta}a\left(\partial_{x}\zeta,\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\zeta\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}+\frac{1}{2}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}.

Next by H
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
lder’s inequality and lemma
2.2
, we can deduce

\displaystyle\left|a\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}\partial_{x}u}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz,\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\right|\leq\|\partial_{y}u\|_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{v}}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{3}{2}+})}\|\sqrt{a}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{v},\Psi}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{7})}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}.

Notice that the following equation holds for any
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
,

(4.3)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\int_{y}^{\infty}fdz\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{v},\Psi}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma})}&\leq\|\int_{y}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{(y-z)^{2}}{8\langle t\rangle}}e^{\frac{z^{2}}{8\langle t\rangle}}\|f(\cdot,z)\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\sigma}}dz\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{v}}}\\
&\leq\|e^{-\frac{y^{2}}{8\langle t\rangle}}\|_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{v}}}\|f\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\sigma,0}}\leq C\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\|f\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\sigma,0}}.\end{aligned}

Combine (
3.14
),(
4.3
) and lemma
4.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|a\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}\partial_{x}u}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz,\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}},

here we use the fact that
\alpha\leq\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4}
.

Noticing that
Q(\xi)=\xi(1+\xi^{2})^{-\frac{2}{3}},
we can obtain the following in the same way,

\displaystyle\left|a\left(\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}\partial_{x}D_{x}u}Q(D_{x})\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz,\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\right|\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}.

On the other hand, since
\partial_{x}u+\partial_{y}v=0,
, we can use H
\mathrm{\ddot{o}}
lder’s inequality, lemma
2.2
and lemma
4.1
to obtain that

\displaystyle\left|a\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}v}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{y}v\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{v}}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}+})}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}.

Next since
\partial_{t}\Psi+2(\partial_{y}\Psi)^{2}=0
, using integrating by parts and Cauchy’s inequality we can get

(4.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&~~~\left(\partial_{t}\mathcal{U}-\partial_{y}^{2}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\dfrac{d}{dt}\|\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}-\left(\partial_{t}\Psi,\mathcal{U}^{2}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\|\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\left(\partial_{y}\mathcal{U},2\mathcal{U}\partial_{y}\Psi\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2}\dfrac{d}{dt}\|\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}-\left(\partial_{t}\Psi,\mathcal{U}^{2}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}-\left(2(\partial_{y}\Psi)^{2},\mathcal{U}^{2}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\dfrac{d}{dt}\|\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}.\end{split}

then combine (
3.6
), we can deduce

(4.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}a(\mathcal{L}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U})_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}&\geq\frac{1}{2}\dfrac{d}{dt}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}-\frac{1}{2}\|\sqrt{a^{\prime}}\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\lambda\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+a\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{u}\partial_{x}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+a\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\\
&+a\left(\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{D_{x}u}Q(D_{x})\partial_{x}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}.\end{split}

Using (
3.14
), lemma
2.4
and lemma
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left|a\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{u}\partial_{x}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{v}}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{3}{2}+})}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}.

By lemma
2.2
and lemma
4.1
, it gives

\displaystyle\left|a\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{v}}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}+})}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-2\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\frac{1}{100}\eta\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\frac{1}{100}\eta\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}},

here we use the fact that
\alpha\leq\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4}
and
\gamma_{0}>1
.

Similarly, applying lemma
2.2
and lemma
4.1
yields

\displaystyle\left|a\left(\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{D_{x}u}Q(D_{x})\partial_{x}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{v}}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{3}{2}+})}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}.

Combining all the above estimates and integrating over
[0,t]
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{a}

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}-\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{a^{\prime}}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds+(1-\frac{1}{50}\eta)\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds

\displaystyle+2\left(\lambda-C(1+\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}})\right)\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}(s)\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds\leq\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}(s)\|\sqrt{a}\zeta(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}ds.

This completes the proof of proposition
4.1
.
∎

Using proposition
4.1
, we can give a proof of proposition
3.1
.

Proof of Proposition
3.1
:

Taking
a(t)=\langle t\rangle^{2l_{\kappa}-2\eta}
in (
4.1
),where
l_{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa(2-\kappa)}{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].
Using lemma
2.7
gives

\displaystyle-(2l_{\kappa}-2\eta)\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds+(1-\frac{1}{50}\eta)\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(-4l_{\kappa}+4\eta)\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds+(1-\frac{1}{50}\eta)\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\eta\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds,

then we have

\displaystyle\|\langle t\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\mathcal{U}(t)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\eta\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds

\displaystyle+2

\displaystyle\left(\lambda-C(1+\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}})\right)\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}(s)\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\mathcal{U}(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds\leq\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}(s)\|\langle s\rangle^{l_{\kappa}-\eta}\zeta(s)\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}ds.

Take
\epsilon_{1}<1
sufficiently small such that when
\epsilon<\epsilon_{1}
, we have
1+\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}<2.
Take
\lambda_{1}>1
sufficiently large, and
\lambda
satisfies

\lambda>\lambda_{1}>4C.
So proposition
3.1
holds.

\square

5.
The Gevrey Estimates of
\zeta
and
\tilde{\zeta}

In this section, we will derive a priori estimates for
\zeta
and
\tilde{\zeta}
. Since the two derivations are similar, we only give the proof procedure for
\tilde{\zeta}
here. Using (
3.1
), (
3.1
) and (
3.13
), we can obtain that
\tilde{\zeta}
satisfies the following equation,

(5.1)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{L_{\kappa}}\tilde{\zeta}=&-B+\dot{\theta}N_{\Phi}+\frac{1-\kappa}{\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}+\left[\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b};\mathcal{L_{\kappa}}\right]\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz\\
&+\frac{2(1-\kappa)}{3\dot{\theta}}\delta(t)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}D_{x}b}Q(D_{x})\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}+\left[\frac{2\delta(t)}{3\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}D_{x}b}Q(D_{x});\mathcal{L_{\kappa}}\right]\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz.\end{split}

By (
3.1
), we can get

(5.2)

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}+\lambda\dot{\theta}(t)[D_{x}]^{\frac{2}{3}}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz=0.

By taking
L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}(\mathbb{R})
inner product of (
5.2
) with
\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz
, we observe

\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}(t,\cdot,z)dz\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathrm{h}}}\leq 0.

Since
\mathcal{U}|_{t=0}=0,
so
\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}(t,x,z)dz=0.
Thus we can obtain
\tilde{\zeta}
satisfying the following initial boundary values

(5.3)

\displaystyle\tilde{\zeta}|_{y=0}=\lim_{y\rightarrow+\infty}\tilde{\zeta}=0,~~\tilde{\zeta}|_{t=0}=b_{\Phi}|_{t=0}=e^{\delta[D_{x}]^{\frac{2}{3}}}b_{0}.

To complete the a priori estimates we also need estimates of the source term
(A,B)
of the equation (
3.1
).

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
(A,B)
be given by (
3.7
) and (
3.8
), respectively, and
\kappa\in(0,2)
. Then for any
s>0,
we have

\displaystyle\|(A,B)\|_{H_{\Psi}^{s,0}}\leq

\displaystyle C\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\|(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{5}{2}+,0}}\|(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{s+\frac{1}{3},0}}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\|(G_{\Phi},\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{5}{2}+,0}}\|(\partial_{y}u_{\Phi},\partial_{y}b_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{s-\frac{1}{3},0}}\right).

The proof of this lemma can be found in lemma 4.2 of
[
26
]
, the only thing to note is that our lemma
2.5
and lemma
2.6
are slightly different from
[
26
]
.

Next we can give the estimates of
(\zeta,\tilde{\zeta})
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,2)
be a given constant. Let
(\zeta,\tilde{\zeta})
be defined by (
3.13
), and
a(t)
be a non-negative and non-decreasing function on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Then when
\alpha\leq\min\{\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4},\frac{2}{3}\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{8}\}
and
\epsilon<\epsilon_{*}

(
\epsilon_{*}
is a sufficiently small number
)
, for any
t<T_{*}
and sufficiently small
\eta>0
the following inequality holds,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &~~~~~\|\sqrt{a}(\zeta(t),\tilde{\zeta}(t))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}-\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{a^{\prime}}(\zeta,\tilde{\zeta})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds\\
&~~~~~+(4l_{\kappa}-\frac{1}{10}\eta)\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{a}(\partial_{y}\zeta,\partial_{y}\tilde{\zeta})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}ds\\
&~~~~~+2\left(\lambda-C(1+\lambda\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}+\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}})\right)\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}(\zeta,\tilde{\zeta})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}ds\\
&\leq\|\sqrt{a}(0)(u_{\Phi}(0),b_{\Phi}(0))\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\frac{2}{25}\eta\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}ds\\
&~~~~~+C(1+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda)\int_{0}^{t}\dot{\theta}\left(\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\|\sqrt{a}(P_{\Phi},N_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\right)ds,\end{aligned}

where
l_{\kappa}=\frac{\kappa(2-\kappa)}{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].

Proof.

By taking
H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}
inner product of (
5.1
) with
a(t)\tilde{\zeta}
, we get

(5.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&a\left(\mathcal{L_{\kappa}}\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\\
=&-a\left(B,\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+a\left(\dot{\theta}N_{\Phi},\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+a\left(\frac{1-\kappa}{\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U},\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\\
&+a\left(\left[\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b};\mathcal{L_{\kappa}}\right]\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz,\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+a\left(\frac{2(1-\kappa)}{3\dot{\theta}}\delta(t)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}D_{x}b}Q(D_{x})\partial_{y}\mathcal{U},\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\\
&+a\left(\left[\frac{2\delta(t)}{3\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}D_{x}b}Q(D_{x});\mathcal{L_{\kappa}}\right]\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz,\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\\
=&\sum_{i=1}^{6}F_{i}.\end{split}

Now start estimating the right side of (
5.5
) term by term.

For
F_{1}
, using (
3.15
) and lemma
5.1
, we have

\displaystyle~~~~~\left|a\left(B,\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\right|\leq\|\sqrt{a}B\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\|(\partial_{y}G_{\Phi},\partial_{y}\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{5}{2}+,0}}\|\sqrt{a}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\right.

\displaystyle~~~~~+\left.\|(G_{\Phi},\tilde{G}_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{5}{2}+,0}}\|\sqrt{a}(\partial_{y}u_{\Phi},\partial_{y}b_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{20}{3},0}}\right)\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}\|\sqrt{a}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-\gamma_{0}}\|\sqrt{a}(\partial_{y}u_{\Phi},\partial_{y}b_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\right)\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle~~~~~+C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4}}\|\sqrt{a}(\partial_{y}u_{\Phi},\partial_{y}b_{\Phi})\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle~~~~~+\frac{1}{100}\eta\|\sqrt{a}(\partial_{y}u_{\Phi},\partial_{y}b_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+C\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-2\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}},

For the last inequality, we use the Cauchy’s inequality and (
3.14
). On the other hand since
\alpha\leq\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4}
and
\gamma>1
, we can deduce

\displaystyle\left|a\left(B,\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\right|\leq

\displaystyle C(\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}+\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}})\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}+C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}(u_{\Phi},b_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{100}\eta\|\sqrt{a}(\partial_{y}u_{\Phi},\partial_{y}b_{\Phi})\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}.

For
F_{2}
, it’s obvious that

\displaystyle\left|a\left(\dot{\theta}N_{\Phi},\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}N_{\Phi}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}+\frac{1}{2}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}.

For
F_{3}
, applying (
3.14
), lemma
2.2
and lemma
4.1
, it gives that

\displaystyle\left|a\left(\frac{1-\kappa}{\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U},\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}\|\partial_{y}b\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{\infty}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}+})}\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-\gamma_{0}-\frac{3}{4}}\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta^{-1}\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{2\alpha-2\gamma_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}+\frac{1}{100}\eta\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{3\alpha-2\gamma_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}+\frac{1}{100}\eta\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta^{-1}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}+\frac{1}{100}\eta\|\sqrt{a}\partial_{y}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{7,0}},

here we use the fact that
\alpha\leq\frac{2}{3}\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{2}
.

For
F_{4}
, using (
3.6
) and direct calculation, we can obtain

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b};\mathcal{L_{\kappa}}\right]=

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}\right)T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b}-\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{t}\partial_{y}b-\kappa\partial_{y}^{3}b}+\lambda\left[T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b};[D_{x}]^{\frac{2}{3}}\right]+\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}\left[T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b};T^{\mathrm{h}}_{u}\partial_{x}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}\left[T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b};T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}\partial_{y}\right]+\frac{2}{3}\delta(t)\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}\left[T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b};T^{\mathrm{h}}_{D_{x}u}Q(D_{x})\partial_{x}\right]+\frac{2\kappa}{\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}^{2}b}\partial_{y}.

Thus we have

a\left(\left[\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b};\mathcal{L_{\kappa}}\right]\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz,\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}=\sum_{i=1}^{7}E_{i}.

Now start to estimate term by term. For
E_{1}
, notice that (
3.14
), so
\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}\right)=\alpha\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-1}
. Using lemma
2.2
, lemma
4.1
and (
4.3
), we can derive

\displaystyle\left|E_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-1}\|\partial_{y}b\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{2}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}+})}\|\sqrt{a}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz\|_{L_{\mathrm{v},\Psi}^{\infty}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{22}{3}})}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\langle t\rangle^{2\alpha-\gamma_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+C\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}},

here we use
\alpha\leq\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}+\frac{5}{8}
. For
E_{2}
, similarly we have

\displaystyle\left|E_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{y}b-\kappa\partial_{y}^{3}b\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{2}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}+})}\|\sqrt{a}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz\|_{L_{\mathrm{v},\Psi}^{\infty}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{22}{3}})}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha+\frac{1}{4}}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{y}b-\kappa\partial_{y}^{3}b\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{2}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}+})}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}.

By (
2.2
) and (
2.4
), we know

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\partial_{y}b-\kappa\partial_{y}^{3}b=

\displaystyle\partial_{y}b\partial_{x}u+\partial_{y}h\partial_{y}u+b\partial_{y}\partial_{x}u+h\partial_{y}^{2}u

\displaystyle-\partial_{y}u\partial_{x}b-\partial_{y}v\partial_{y}b-u\partial_{y}\partial_{x}b-v\partial_{y}^{2}b.

To handle the product, we need to use the following basic fact of Sobolev space: let
s>\frac{1}{2},
then

(5.6)

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}\leq C\|f\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}\|g\|_{H_{\mathrm{h}}^{s}}.

Noticing that
\partial_{y}v=-\partial_{x}u,\partial_{y}h=-\partial_{x}b
, at this time we can use lemma
4.1
to get the following,

\displaystyle~~~~~\|\partial_{t}\partial_{y}b-\kappa\partial_{y}^{3}b\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{2}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}+})}

\displaystyle\leq\|(u,b)\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{\infty}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}+})}\|(\partial_{y}u,\partial_{y}b)\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+,0}}+\|(v,h)\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{\infty}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}+})}\|(\partial_{y}^{2}u,\partial_{y}^{2}b)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}+,0}}

\displaystyle~~~~~+\|(\partial_{y}u,\partial_{y}b)\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{\infty}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}+})}\|(u,b)\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+,0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-2\gamma_{0}-\frac{3}{4}}.

Thus
E_{2}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|E_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-2\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{2\alpha-2\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+C\epsilon\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}},

here we use
\alpha\leq\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4}
. For
E_{3}
,

\displaystyle\left|E_{3}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\|\partial_{y}b\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{2}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{3}{2}+})}\|\sqrt{a}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz\|_{L_{\mathrm{v},\Psi}^{\infty}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{22}{3}})}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\lambda\langle t\rangle^{-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}},

here we also use
\alpha\leq\gamma_{0}+\frac{1}{4}
. For
E_{4}
, by
(
3.14
), (
4.3
), lemma
2.3
and lemma
4.1
, it’s easy to know

\displaystyle\left|E_{4}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\alpha}\|\partial_{y}b\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{2}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{3}{2}+})}\|u\|_{L_{\mathrm{v}}^{\infty}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{3}{2}+})}\|\sqrt{a}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz\|_{L_{\mathrm{v},\Psi}^{\infty}(H_{\mathrm{h}}^{\frac{22}{3}})}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{2\alpha-2\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+C\epsilon\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}.

For
E_{5}
, first we notice

(5.7)

\displaystyle\left[T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}u};T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}\partial_{y}\right]=\left[T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}u};T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}\right]\partial_{y}-T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}^{2}u}.

Applying (
5.7
), lemma
2.2
, lemma
2.3
and lemma
4.1
, we can get the estimate of
E_{5}
,

\displaystyle\left|E_{5}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|a\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}\left(\left[T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}b};T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}\right]\mathcal{U},\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\right|+\left|a\frac{1}{\dot{\theta}}\left(T^{\mathrm{h}}_{v}T^{\mathrm{h}}_{\partial_{y}^{2}u}\int_{y}^{\infty}\mathcal{U}dz,\tilde{\zeta}\right)_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{2\alpha-2\gamma_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+C\epsilon\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}.

For
E_{6}
, using a similar calculation as
E_{4}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|E_{6}\right|\leq C\epsilon\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\mathcal{U}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{22}{3},0}}+C\epsilon\dot{\theta}\|\sqrt{a}\tilde{\zeta}\|^{2}_{H_{\Psi}^{\frac{23}{3},0}}.

For
E_{7}
